✻ Cooked for 3s · done 11:49 AM

⏺ Agent "QA confirm and close #120" finished · 45s

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #89 is implemented and committed on `issue-89-recursive-history`, cut
  from main be34c02 (current head; the task said 090cd3e). Head commit is
  38f5122, in worktree /Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/wt-issue-89. Not
  pushed or merged. I posted `## Engineer: ready for QA` on #89:
  https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/89#issuecomment-6261516480

  **Implementation** (`backend/gormstore.go`)
  - `gormStore.WalkFirstParents` is one recursive CTE with no per-commit
  queries.
  - A single function, `firstParentSQL`, holds the per-dialect difference, with
  a comment. Postgres uses `encode(..., 'escape')` plus the pgx regex. SQLite
  uses `CAST AS TEXT`, `GLOB`, and a fixed-offset `substr`.
  - Both read only the first 100 bytes of the body, so a `parent <40-hex>` line
  in the message is ignored. #90 can reuse `firstParentSQL`.
  - `limit < 1` returns an empty non-nil list. A missing tip or a missing middle
  parent wraps `treebuild.ErrNotFound`.
  - Postgres gotcha: `objects.oid` is `char(40)`, so the parent is cast to
  `char(40)` to use the primary key. Without the cast a 10000-deep walk took
  about 30s. The pgx store hits the same slowness and I left it unchanged.
  - The CTE no longer carries the bodies; they are joined once at the end. This
  cut the SQLite 10k walk from about 5s to well under a second.

  **Tests**
  - `backend/history_test.go` has `TestWalkFirstParents` (the store named by
  `TEST_STORE`) and `TestWalkFirstParentsGorm` (gormStore on both sqlite and
  postgres, whatever `TEST_STORE` is).
  - They cover the 500-commit chain in order, `limit`, `limit < 1`, missing tip,
  non-commit tip, missing middle parent, and the message-parent line with
  multi-byte text at the window edge. The depth cap is checked with a
  10005-commit chain and `limit` 10000, which returns exactly 10000. That check
  is skipped for the pgx store only.
  - `backend/auth_test.go` is outside the file list the issue names; I edited it
  only to remove the #89 sqlite skips. `TestReadEndpointsGolden` and
  `TestReadsRacingRepoDeleteAre404` now run and pass under sqlite.
  `TestCommitLog*` and `TestRenameRepo` still skip for #91.
  - gofmt and `go vet` are clean. `go test -p 1 -race ./...` passes with
  `TEST_STORE` postgres (default), memory and sqlite, against Postgres on
  :54329.

  **Open point for QA:** the HTTP-level `TestCommitLog*` tests cannot run on
  sqlite until #91 (CreateRef) lands. The new store-level tests carry the sqlite
  coverage in the meantime, so the "0 skips for sqlite" criterion holds for
  this issue's own tests, not for those three.

  Process step 4 was not needed; I found no contradiction with plan.md.

  int4". sqlite handles these fine, and the pgx variant is fine. The gorm CTE

  (history.go caps page*per_page at 10000), so low severity. Fix direction: cast
  the limit to bigint in the postgres variant, then add a store test with a
  huge limit on both dialects.

  firstParentSQL.

  WalkFirstParents itself has no cap; a direct call with limit 20000 on a
  10005-chain returns 10005. The "depth cap" store test passes 10000 explicitly,
  so it checks the limit, not the cap.

  unknown repo: all ErrNotFound. Merge with a missing second parent walks fine;

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #91 is implemented and committed on branch `issue-91-gorm-branches`,
  head `77811a3`, in worktree /Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/wt-issue-91.
  The branch was cut from `main` at 3616552 (local `main` includes #88). Nothing
  is pushed or merged. I posted `## Engineer: ready for QA` on the issue:
  https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/91#issuecomment-6261589487

  Tests: gofmt and `go vet` are clean, and `go test -p 1 -race ./...` passes
  with TEST_STORE=memory, sqlite and postgres (:54329). The branch tests run
  with 0 skips on sqlite.

  What changed:
  - **`backend/gormstore.go`:** `ListRefs`, `CreateRef`, `DeleteRef` and
  `DeleteNonDefaultRef` are ported.
    - `ListRefs` sorts bytewise (SQLite's default order, `COLLATE "C"` on
  Postgres) and returns `[]` rather than nil.
    - `CreateRef` returns `ErrInvalidOID` for a non-40-hex oid before any write,
  and `ErrConflict` for an existing name.
    - `DeleteRef` returns `ErrNotFound` for a missing branch.
    - `DeleteNonDefaultRef` decides "is default" inside the DELETE itself. A
  missing branch gives `ErrNotFound` and the default gives `errDefaultBranch`.
  - **`auth_test.go`:** the #91 entries are removed from `sqlitePending`, and
  `TestEmptyFlag` keeps only its #92 reason.
  - **Tests:** new `TestStoreBranchOps` in `backend/branches_test.go` covers all
  stores, including a non-ASCII sort order and default-branch protection after
  moving the default. The bad-oid check in it runs on gormStore only.
  - **Two small test fixes:** `TestStoreSentinels` in `storage_test.go` now uses
  a valid 40-hex oid, and `TestGormStoreUnportedMethods` in `gormstore_test.go`
  now checks `WalkFirstParents` instead of `ListRefs`.

  Things to know:
  - **Merge conflict with #89:** I added `TestPrivateRepoReadShapes` back to
  `sqlitePending` as "#89 (history; run)". Its `commits?ref=` subtest was
  blocked by #91 first and now fails on sqlite only because history isn't
  ported. When #89 merges, it should delete that entry. Both branches edit that
  map, so expect a small conflict there. The #89-related edits to `gormstore.go`
  should be in a different region.
  - **Bad oids on pgx and memory:** the up-front `ErrInvalidOID` check applies
  to gormStore only. The pgx and memory stores still leave a bad oid to their
  foreign-key check, as before, since changing that would be HTTP-behaviour
  scope.
  - **No separate concurrency test:** I didn't write a new one for the "default
  changes between check and delete" case. The existing
  `TestDeleteBranchRacingSetDefault` runs on sqlite now that it's unblocked, and
  `TestStoreBranchOps` covers a default that moves between deletes.

  Issue #90 is implemented and committed on `issue-90-portable-ancestry` at head
  `10134e8`, in worktree `/Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/wt-issue-90`, cut
  from origin/main 3976e7f. Nothing is pushed, and I posted `## Engineer: ready
  for QA` on the issue.

  The sqlite acceptance criterion is only partly met. Six tests still skip on
  sqlite until #91 lands CreateRef: `TestCommitReachabilityParents`,
  `TestCompare`, `TestMergeEqualTipsUpToDate`, `TestMergeErrors`,
  `TestMergeFastForward` and `TestMergeTipMoved`. I removed only the `#90
  (ancestry; read)` text from their `sqlitePending` entries in
  `backend/auth_test.go`, so their reasons now read `#91 (CreateRef; run)`. The
  reachability behaviour itself does run on sqlite, through new tests that put
  objects in directly and don't need refs.

  - **Implementation:** `gormStore.CommitReachability` in `backend/gormstore.go`
  is one recursive statement for sqlite and Postgres. A missing or non-commit
  oid or parent gives `false`, not an error.
  - **Parent extraction:** `firstParentSQL` is now `parentSQL(g, 0)`, and the
  new `parentSQL(g, k)` reads the k-th parent with the same header-only rule. It
  is a generalisation, not a second extraction, and its output for k=0 is
  unchanged. A merge commit's second parent sits past #89's 100-byte window, so
  `firstParentSQL` alone could not see it.
  - **Octopus cap:** the walk follows at most 16 parents per commit
  (`maxReachParents`), documented in the code.
  - **Tests:** in `backend/merge_reach_test.go`, one suite runs on gormStore
  sqlite and Postgres and on the `TEST_STORE` store. It covers identical,
  fast-forward both ways, diverged, two-parent merge, octopus, a fake parent
  line in the message, missing and tree oids, a missing parent, and a 1000-deep
  chain through a merge. The suite skips the 1000-deep part on the pgx store,
  which exceeds its statement timeout under `-race`.
  - **Statement count:** `TestGormCommitReachabilityStatementCount` (Postgres
  only) shows 5-commit and 500-commit chains each take 1 statement.
  - **Results:** gofmt and vet are clean. `go test -p 1 -race ./...` passes for
  `TEST_STORE` postgres, memory and sqlite.
  - **#91 conflict risk:** in `auth_test.go` I touched only the six lines above.
  In `gormstore.go`, I removed the `CommitReachability` stub and replaced
  `firstParentSQL` with `firstParentSQL` plus `parentSQL`, `maxReachParents` and
  `CommitReachability`.

  newest first"). Reproduced deterministically on the shared mg DB. Root cause
  is test fragility, not this branch: the test lists unfiltered /repos (page of
  50) and its repos use a fixed past clock. Once the shared DB has about 47+
  public repos, its oldest repo falls off page 1. The branch does not touch pgx
  store or repos_test.go.

  my qa92* users and repos. After that, TestListRepos passes on the shared DB

  scratchpad/qa92-count10-postgres-rerun.log, status in
  scratchpad/qa92-status2.txt.

  Minor parity note (not a leak): memStore uses strings.EqualFold, so Kelvin
  sign U+212A matches "k". pg (C locale) and sqlite lower() do not. Usernames
  are [a-z0-9-] only, so this only affects non-ASCII owner queries on
  STORE=memory. Also, negative LIMIT or OFFSET differs across stores but is
  unreachable via HTTP.

› Message from general-purpose
  The 16-parent cap is fixed on `issue-90-portable-ancestry` at head `5b70f6a`.
  `gormStore.CommitReachability` now follows every parent, and I posted the new
  `## Engineer: ready for QA` comment on #90. Nothing is pushed.

  - **How it works:** `maxReachParents` is removed. The one recursive statement
  walks (root, oid, k) rows and reads parent k of each commit, so there is no
  fixed limit. Parent 0 uses `firstParentSQL`. Later parents use the new
  `nextParentSQL`, which reads the fixed 48-byte header line at 47+48k. A parent
  that isn't stored skips only itself.
  - **Statement count:** it is still one statement. The 5-commit and 500-commit
  chains take 1 each, checked by the Postgres statement-count test.
  - **New tests:** `reachSuite` in `backend/merge_reach_test.go` now has octopus
  merges of 16, 17, 18, 20 and 40 parents. For each, the target is reached via
  the first, middle and last parent, plus reversed, an unrelated commit, and a
  missing first parent. It runs on gormStore sqlite and Postgres and on the
  `TEST_STORE` store, including pgx, which gives the same answers.
  - **Speed:** the reachability tests under `-race` take about 3s on the gorm
  side, down from about 11s before. I did not run a 10000-deep chain, only the
  existing 1000-deep case.
  - **Checks:** gofmt and vet are clean. `go test -p 1 -race ./...` passes for
  `TEST_STORE` postgres, memory and sqlite.

✻ Churned for 36s · done 12:29 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #122 is fixed on branch `issue-122-pg-default-branch-race` in
  `/Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/wt-issue-122`, head commit `fecb8bb`, cut
  from f9ee90d. Nothing is pushed or merged. I posted `## Engineer: ready for
  QA` on the issue.

  **Fix:**
  - `pgxStore.DeleteNonDefaultRef` and `pgxStore.UpdateRepo` (the latter only
  when `default_branch` is set) now run in a transaction that first takes
  `SELECT 1 FROM repos WHERE id=$1 FOR UPDATE`. This goes through a new
  `lockRepo` helper in `backend/store.go`.
  - In `backend/gormstore.go`, `DeleteNonDefaultRef` and `UpdateRepo` take the
  same lock through a new `lockRepoRow` helper. It does nothing on SQLite.
  - Memory and SQLite are unchanged, and error mapping is unchanged.

  **New test:** `TestDeleteNonDefaultRefRacingSetDefault` in
  `backend/default_branch_race_test.go`.
  - **Stores:** it runs on memory, sqlite, gorm-postgres and pgx. It does not
  depend on `TEST_STORE`, because gorm on Postgres is not otherwise reachable
  through `testServer`. The two Postgres stores each get a fresh schema and need
  `TEST_DATABASE_URL`.
  - **Method:** 8 repos race in parallel for 40 rounds each. A barrier starts
  the delete and the update together, with no sleeps. The assertion is that the
  default branch always names an existing ref.
  - **Before the fix:** it failed on pgx in 103 of 320 rounds and on
  gorm-postgres in 29 of 320.
  - **After the fix:** it passes with `-count=10 -race`.

  **Verification:** I used an isolated database `mg_e122` on :54329 and dropped
  it afterwards. gofmt and `go vet` are clean, and `go test -p 1 -race ./...` is
  green for TEST_STORE=postgres, memory and sqlite.

  I did not touch `auth_test.go`.

⏺ Agent "QA confirm and close #90" finished · 3m 4s

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #93 is done and ready for QA. It is on branch `issue-93-sqlite-driver`,
  head `9c0b1d1`, in worktree `/Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/wt-issue-93`,
  cut from origin/main 99bc255. Nothing is pushed or merged. I posted `##
  Engineer: ready for QA` on the issue (comment 6262347500).

  - **Opener:** `backend/sqlite.go` adds `OpenSQLite(path)`, which creates the
  data directory and opens SQLite with the pure-Go driver and `sqliteDSNParams`.
  That covers `_txlock=immediate` and the 10s busy timeout. It rejects an empty
  path or one containing `?` or `#`. `CloseGorm` is there too, and the test
  opener now goes through `OpenSQLite`.
  - **Store wiring:** in `backend/main.go`, `openStore` for `DB_DRIVER=sqlite`
  opens the file, runs `Migrate` and returns `gormStore`. The Postgres path is
  unchanged.
  - **`checkStoreAvailable`:** it no longer refuses sqlite and now only rejects
  an unknown driver.

✻ Churned for 20s · done 12:49 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  The deadlock regression on #122 is fixed. Head is now `835bd79` on
  `issue-122-pg-default-branch-race`, rebased onto origin/main 8e0f27f, with
  nothing pushed. I posted a new `## Engineer: ready for QA` comment on the
  issue.

  **Cause:** the `FOR UPDATE` lock on the repos row conflicts with the
  foreign-key check and cascade that commit (`PutObjectsMoveRef`) and
  `DeleteRepo` take on that row. They lock in the opposite order and queue
  behind each other.

  **Fix:** `DeleteNonDefaultRef` and `UpdateRepo(default_branch)` now take
  `pg_advisory_xact_lock(repoID)` in their transaction instead of locking the
  row. This applies to both pgx and gorm on Postgres. Only these two operations
  take the advisory lock, so it adds no lock cycle. The helpers are `lockRepo`
  in `backend/store.go` and `lockRepoRow` in `backend/gormstore.go`. SQLite and
  memory are unchanged.

  **Deadlock counts:** I used the QA probe `TestQA122RepoDelete` on pgx, 200
  rounds per run, in the isolated DB `mg_e122b`. I dropped the DB afterwards.

  | Variant | 40P01 per run |
  |---|---|
  | origin/main, before any fix | 0-1 |
  | `fecb8bb`, row `FOR UPDATE` | 3-13 |
  | `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (tried, rejected) | 0-3 |
  | `835bd79`, advisory lock | 0 1 0 1 1 0 1 0 over 8 runs |

  gorm-postgres stayed at 0 throughout. The mixed-workload probe
  `TestQA122Probe` showed 0 deadlocks.

  **Verification:** `TestDeleteNonDefaultRefRacingSetDefault` still passes with
  `-count=10 -race`. gofmt and vet are clean, and `go test -p 1 -race ./...` is
  green for TEST_STORE postgres, memory and sqlite. I did not commit the probe
  file; it is in the scratchpad as `qa122_probe_test.go.moved`.

  runs `check-env`, then the backend with `DB_DRIVER=postgres`.
  - **`test-all`:** runs the backend suite with `TEST_STORE=memory`, then
  `TEST_STORE=sqlite`, then `make test` (Postgres on :54329, then the frontend).
  - **`clean-data`:** deletes only `data/mg.db`, `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal`.
  It refuses an unsafe path, a symlinked `data`, or any directory not named
  `data` under the repo. It never uses `rm -r`, keeps other files in `data/`,
  and removes the directory only if it is then empty.

  I ran each target:
  - **`run-sqlite`:** on a fresh tree it generated `.env` (mode 600), started
  the server, created `data/mg.db` with its `-wal` and `-shm` files, and
  listened on the port.

  `.env` unchanged.
  - **`clean-data`:** it removed only the db files and kept an extra
  `data/other.txt`. A second run said there was nothing to remove.
  - **`run-pg`:** without `DATABASE_URL` it stops at `check-env`.
  - **`test-all`:** the memory, sqlite and Postgres backend suites all pass. The
  frontend step fails with "vitest: command not found" because the new worktree

  pushed or merged.

  **Changes**
  - `backend/gormrefs.go`: `sqliteDSNParams` now also sets
  `synchronous(NORMAL)`. `foreign_keys`, WAL, `busy_timeout` 10000 and
  `_txlock=immediate` were already in the DSN, so the DSN applies all five to
  every pooled connection.
  - `backend/sqlite.go`: updated the doc comment only.
  - `backend/sqlite_pragma_test.go` (new, runs whatever `TEST_STORE` is):
    - Reads `foreign_keys`, `journal_mode`, `synchronous` and `busy_timeout`
  back from 8 pooled connections held open at the same time.
    - Busy handling: a second `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` blocks while the first
  transaction holds the write lock, then succeeds with no `SQLITE_BUSY`.
    - 100 parallel first commits to 100 different repos all return 201.
  - `_docs/plan.md` Storage section: the write limit is now documented as one
  writer at a time through SQLite's write lock plus the 10 s busy timeout, with
  readers unblocked under WAL.

  **Deviation to know about**
  - The plan text used to say "a single writer connection". I did not cap the
  pool at one connection, because that would deadlock a write on the outer store
  while a transaction is open (the QA note on #88/#93 describes that case). I
  changed the plan text to match, and the issue comment says so.

  **Results**
  - `gofmt` and `go vet` are clean.
  - `go test -p 1 -race -count=1 ./...` is green for `TEST_STORE=postgres` (on
  the isolated `mg_e95` DB, which I dropped afterwards), `memory` and `sqlite`.

  **Issue comments**
  - `gh issue view --comments` printed nothing, so I read the issue through `gh
  api`. The issue has no PM-groomed comment.

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #97 is implemented and committed on `issue-97-store-matrix` (head
  `2141618`, cut from origin/main 249a028, in
  /Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/wt-issue-97). It is not pushed. I posted
  `## Engineer: ready for QA` on the issue:
  https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/97#issuecomment-6262788742

  What it does:
  - **Matrix runner:** `scripts/test-matrix.sh`, run by `make test-all`, runs
  `go test -p 1 -race ./...` on memory, sqlite and postgres in turn. It prints
  one pass/fail/skip line per store. You can pass store names to run only those.
  - **Postgres isolation:** the postgres pass creates its own database
  `mg_matrix_<pid>` on `TEST_DATABASE_URL` or the :54329 server, applies
  `backend/db/schema.sql` to it, and drops it on exit. I confirmed no
  `mg_matrix_*` databases were left behind.
  - **No silent skips:** an unset URL on the postgres pass now fails the matrix
  (via `TEST_MATRIX_STRICT=1` in `backend/auth_test.go`), and so does an
  unreachable server. Any skipped test not listed for its store in
  `backend/known_skips.txt` also fails. That file is the single place for known
  skips, and `--update-skips` regenerates it. I checked both failure cases by
  hand.
  - **Default store:** with `TEST_STORE` unset, the store is postgres if
  `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set, otherwise sqlite (`backend/storetest_test.go`).
  - **Docs:** `_docs/testing-guidelines.md` now has the matrix section and the
  :54329 URL (no password, no secrets).

  Results from `make test-all` (about 1m32s):

  | Store | Result | Pass | Fail | Skip |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | memory | PASS | 742 | 0 | 32 |
  | sqlite | PASS | 742 | 0 | 32 |
  | postgres | PASS | 808 | 0 | 2 |

  The memory and sqlite skips are the Postgres-only tests, which skip because
  `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is deliberately unset on those passes. The two postgres
  skips are `TestBaseline` and the 10005-commit walk. `sqlitePending` is empty.
  gofmt and vet are clean.

  `test-all` target and its own separate `.PHONY` line, placed after
  `test-fast`. Whoever merges second must keep a single `test-all`. Mine runs
  only the backend matrix; #99's also runs the frontend.

⏺ QA #95 PASS at f503d26:
  https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/95#issuecomment-6262835614

  - Pragmas: foreign_keys=1, journal_mode=wal, synchronous=1 (NORMAL) and
    busy_timeout=10000 read back correctly on 32 pinned connections over 3
    close/reopen rounds. _txlock=immediate is confirmed on each of them.
  - Concurrency: 100 concurrent commits over HTTP on the real binary all
    returned 201, with a 300-request reader loop all returning 200. 50 same-tip
    commits gave 1 201 and 49 409. A real FK violation is rejected 40 of 40
    times.
  - Crash recovery: after kill -9 mid-burst of 200 commits, integrity_check and
    foreign_key_check were ok, and the interrupted requests left no ref.
  - DSN injection: ? and # in the path are refused at startup. Other forms are
    refused or taken literally, and the pragmas are unchanged in every accepted
    case.
  - Busy timeout: with an external BEGIN IMMEDIATE held, the commit fails after
    10.2s with a generic HTTP 500. No error text leaks, but a 503 would be more
    accurate.
  - Plan deviation: the criterion says "(e.g. one writer)", an example, so it is
    not contradicted. But the branch rewrote the plan.md line without logging a
    question first, which the process requires.

  I sent the engineer back for a docs-only follow-up: log the plan.md decision
  in _docs/questions.md with the default used and the alternative. Then a quick
  QA re-check and the reviewer.

  Non-blocking follow-ups from QA: the pragma test doesn't cover recycled
  connections, and a cancelled request context doesn't interrupt a writer
  waiting on BEGIN IMMEDIATE.

  deadlock.

  inside `InTx`.

  `TEST_MATRIX_STRICT` is now described correctly as a guard on the Go side
  only. The choice is logged in `_docs/questions.md` under "Test matrix (#97),
  stashed 2026-10-19", with "unset fails" as the alternative.

  2. **URL parsing:** the parsing moved into a new `scripts/pgurl.sh`.
     - It handles URLs with or without a path, with or without a query string,
  and with user:password. The isolated `mg_matrix_<pid>` database is now always
  used.
     - A URL with no database connects to the `postgres` maintenance database to
  create it.
     - A value that isn't a `postgres://` URL fails the matrix.
     - `scripts/test-pgurl.sh` checks these URL shapes and needs no database.
     - I also ran the real matrix with `postgres://test@localhost:54329` and
  with `.../?sslmode=disable`. Both passed.

  3. **Strict mode and signals:**
     - **No `set -e`/pipefail:** I did not add it. The script counts results
  with `grep -c`, which exits 1 on zero matches, and it records go test's exit
  code itself, so `-e` would cut the summary short. The script has a comment
  saying this.
     - **TERM/INT:** these now stop the `go test` child, and the isolated
  database is still dropped. I checked it: exit code 130, no leftover `go test`
  process, no `mg_matrix_*` database.

› Message from general-purpose
  Issue #113 is implemented and committed on branch
  `issue-113-sqlite-integration-smoke`, head `b53a8c4`, in
  `/Users/akniyetislambekkyzy/Desktop/wt-issue-113`. It is not pushed or merged.
  I posted the `## Engineer: ready for QA` comment:
  https://github.com/L0nelyG0d/micro-git/issues/113#issuecomment-6263250945

  **What was added**
  - **`make dev-sqlite`:** starts the backend on SQLite (`data/mg.db`) and Vite
  with the `/api` proxy and `VITE_USE_MOCK` unset, and Ctrl-C stops both. The
  two-command alternative is `make run-sqlite` plus `make frontend`. It takes
  `PORT=` and `VITE_PORT=` overrides.
  - **`make smoke-sqlite` (`scripts/smoke-sqlite.sh`):** picks free ports and a
  temp dir, builds the backend and starts it on a temp SQLite file with a random
  `JWT_SECRET`, and starts Vite. Everything goes through the Vite port only:
    - **SPA:** `/` and a deep link are served.
    - **Auth and cookies:** register sets no cookie. Login sets the `session`
  cookie as HttpOnly, `Path=/`, not Secure over http.
    - **Journey:** create repo, two commits, a stale tip returns 409 with
  `current_tip`, then history, tree, blob, commit detail and repo list.
    - **Reload and logout:** a reload with the same cookie jar is still logged
  in; logout, 401, and login again also work.
    - **Persistence:** a backend restart on the same file keeps the session and
  the history.
  - **`frontend/src/services/parity.live.test.ts`:** runs one 26-step journey
  through `MockApiService` and the real `HttpApiService` against the live stack.
  It compares JSON shapes and error status and message. It is skipped unless
  `SMOKE_BASE_URL` is set, which the script does.
  - **Docs:** new `_docs/iterations.md` with the steps and ports, plus a README
  pointer.

  **Results**
  - `make smoke-sqlite` passed 43 of 43, and the mock-versus-real check found no
  differences.
  - Frontend `npm test -- --run` passed 374 tests (30 files, 1 skipped), and
  `tsc -b` and `npm run build` are clean.
  - I checked `make dev-sqlite PORT=18123 VITE_PORT=15173` by hand:
  `/api/health` returned 200 through the proxy and the SPA was served.
  - No Go files were touched, so I did not run the Go suites for memory, sqlite
  or postgres.

  **Findings**
  - I found no integration bugs. Cookie settings, the proxy, and response shapes
  all match `openapi.yaml`.
  - Port 5173 is held on this machine by a Vite process from the main checkout,
  which is why `dev-sqlite` needs `VITE_PORT`. `make dev` and `make frontend`
  hardcode 5173.
  - `_docs/iterations.md` did not exist; #106 also plans it, so expect a trivial
  merge conflict and keep both sections.
  - Out of scope: the browser E2E (#107) and a pixel-level UI comparison. The
  parity check compares response shapes only.

  130'` exits before `rm -rf`. Contradicts the "removed on exit" claim in
  iterations.md. Leaked copies are in `qa113-leftover` and `qa113-leak-O5Llak`

  3. `make frontend` cannot be overridden from the command line, so the
  two-command path fails whenever 5173 is taken (it is busy on this machine,
  held by another checkout that I did not touch).
  4. Every anonymous load logs a 401 for `/api/auth/me` in the browser console.

  **Judgment call:** the verdict is PASS because all three acceptance criteria
  pass. Finding 1 is a real defect against the smoke's own stated cleanup
  guarantee, so if you treat the cleanup check as a gate, change this to FAIL.
